A     Technical lemmas
Lemma A.1. Define Ω := (0, 1)d . Suppose fn ⇀ f weakly in L2 (Ω), and each fn is monotone
nondecreasing (resp. monotone nonincreasing). Then f is monotone nondecreasing (resp. monotone
nonincreasing).

Proof. Recall that for each ε > 0 and g ∈ L2 (Ω), g ε = ηε ∗ g is the mollification of g, defined on
Ωε = (ε, 1 − ε)d .
    Let ε ∈ (0, 1/3). We claim that fnε → f ε pointwise in Ωε . Indeed, fix any x ∈ Ωε and define
ηε,x ∈ C ∞ (Rd ) by ηε,x (y) := ηε (x − y). Since fn ⇀ f weakly in L2 (Ω), we obtain
         Z                                                            Z
 ε
f (x) =     ηε (x − y)f (y) dy = ⟨ηε,x , f ⟩ = lim ⟨ηε,x , fn ⟩ = lim   ηε (x − y)fn (y) dy = lim fnε (x) .
          Ω                                  n→∞               n→∞ Ω                          n→∞

    Now, suppose without loss of generality that each fn is monotone nondecreasing. Let ε ∈
(0, 1/3). It is immediate that each fnε is also monotone nondecreasing (in Ωε ). Since fnε → f ε
pointwise in Ωε , we conclude that f ε is monotone nondecreasing as well.
    The conclusion follows the fact that f ε → f almost everywhere as ε → 0.

    The following lemma is essentially standard, and below we present the proof sketched in [Fen].

Lemma A.2. Let Ω ⊂ Rd be an open set, let u ∈ L2 (Ω) and let (un )n∈N be a bounded sequence in
L2 (Ω) such that un → u in L2loc (Ω). Then un ⇀ u weakly in L2 (Ω).

Proof. It suffices to show that every subsequence (unk )k∈N has a subsequence that weakly converges
to u. Fix any subsequence (unk )k∈N . First, since unk → u in L2loc (Ω), we have that ⟨unk , ϕ⟩ → ⟨u, ϕ⟩
for all ϕ ∈ Cc∞ (Ω). Moreover, since L2 (Ω) is a Hilbert space and (unk )k∈N is bounded, let (unkℓ )ℓ∈N



                                                    83
be a subsequence such that unkℓ ⇀ w weakly in L2 (Ω) for some w ∈ L2 (Ω). Now, let v ∈ L2 (Ω).
Since Cc∞ (Ω) is dense in L2 (Ω), let (ϕm )m∈N be a sequence in Cc∞ (Ω) with ϕm → v in L2 (Ω). Then

         ⟨u, v⟩ = lim ⟨u, ϕm ⟩                                   (Since ϕm → v in L2 (Ω))
                 m→∞
               = lim lim ⟨unk , ϕm ⟩                                  (As observed above)
                m→∞ k→∞
                         D           E
               = lim lim unkℓ , ϕm                (Taking subsequence preserves the limit)
                 m→∞ ℓ→∞
               = lim ⟨w, ϕm ⟩                           (Since unkℓ ⇀ w weakly in L2 (Ω))
                 m→∞
               = ⟨w, v⟩                                          (Since ϕm → v in L2 (Ω))
                      D       E
               = lim unkℓ , v                           (Since unkℓ ⇀ w weakly in L2 (Ω)),
                 ℓ→∞

so unkℓ ⇀ u weakly in L2 (Ω) as needed.

Lemma A.3 (From “almost Lipschitz” to Lipschitz). Let M ∈ R≥0 and let N ⊂ I be a measure
zero set. Suppose f : I → R satisfies |f (x) − f (y)| ≤ M |x − y| for all x, y ∈ I \ N . Then there
exists a M -Lipschitz function g : I → R such that f = g in I \ N .

Proof. Let f : I → (−∞, +∞] be given by f := f in I \ N and f := +∞ in N . Define g : I → R by
                                   (
                                     f (x)             if x ∈ I \ N
                           g(x) :=
                                     lim inf z→x f (z) otherwise.

Note that g is real-valued because, when x ∈ N , we may find a sequence (zn )n∈N ⊂ I \ N such that
zn → x and then, by the definition of lim inf by subsequential limits and the assumption on f ,
                                                                         
g(x) = lim inf f (z) ≤ lim f (zn ) = lim f (zn ) ≤ lim f (z1 )+M |zn −z1 | = f (z1 )+M |x−z1 | < +∞ .
        z→x          n→∞           n→∞            n→∞

Clearly g = f in I \ N . We claim that g is M -Lipschitz. By the assumption on f , we do have
|g(x) − g(y)| ≤ M |x − y| for all x, y ∈ I \ N . Now, let x ∈ N and y ∈ I \ N . By the definition of
g, fix a sequence (zn )n∈N ⊂ I \ N such that zn → x and moreover limn→∞ f (zn ) = g(x). Then

     |g(x) − g(y)| = lim f (zn ) − f (y) = lim |f (zn ) − f (y)| ≤ lim M |zn − y| = M |x − y| ,
                      n→∞                   n→∞                   n→∞

as desired. The case when x, y ∈ N follows by the triangle inequality: suppose x < y without loss
of generality, choose any z ∈ (x, y) \ N , which is possible since N has measure zero, and use the
above to conclude that

         |g(x) − g(y)| ≤ |g(x) − g(z)| + |g(z) − g(y)| ≤ M |x − z| + M |z − y| = M |x − y| .
